## P0.4 B5 — WB + Outline Guided-Start Playbooks + Character Psych Module — Load-Bearing Decisions

Extends B0-B4's guided-start UX (composer blurb + Guided Setup button + Light/Standard/Grill-me style dropdown, `chatContextService.ts`'s style-hint pattern) to World-Building and Outline chats, plus WB's Character template gets an opt-in psych module (MBTI/Enneagram/freeform blurb, chat-interview-then-propose). User confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` to include Outline in this pass, even though Outline's own locked §4 spec (unlike WB's §1) never explicitly asked for guided-start UI — done for parity across all three playbook-style chats, at the user's explicit choice. Plan locked via `EnterPlanMode` before implementation (same plan file as B0-B4, `zazzy-wishing-cray.md`, overwritten for this slice).

**Research eliminated most of what looked like new architecture before any code was written.** `lorebookEntries.metadata` is already a generic, unvalidated JSON column (already holding `relationships[]`) — `psychProfile` fits there with **zero schema migration**. The generic `PUT /api/lorebook/:id` route already accepts arbitrary body fields including `metadata` with no allowlist — `useUpdateLorebookMutation` already does everything the psych-proposal Accept path needs, **no new server route**. And `CodexPendingChangesPanel`/`CodexHistoryPanel` in `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx` were the exact precedent for a new read-only `PsychProfilePanel` — rendered outside the RHF form, reading straight off the `entry` prop already passed in, no query of its own.

**Psych module deliberately bypasses the Codex pending-change pipeline entirely, not an extension of it.** `codexService.proposeChange`/`approvePendingChange` is architecturally concrete-state-only — every approve takes a `codexSnapshots` row, which is meant to be a history of the entry's *concrete* state. Extending `codexPendingChanges` with a `proposedMetadata` field would have made psych data show up in Codex snapshot history, blurring the project's own standing line (CLAUDE.md: "Character Codex... concrete/physical state only (no psychological modeling)"; "Keep psychological/thematic enforcement out of scope"). The psych module reconciles with that constraint by staying strictly outside it: a **new parallel `psych-proposal` fence**, same posture as `note-proposal` (ephemeral client-side card, never persisted server-side as a pending row), writing directly to `metadata.psychProfile` via the plain entry-update route — never touching `codexState`, `codexPendingChanges`, or `codexSnapshots`. The design doc's own framing ("writing aid only," "not scanner-enforced," "not a continuity 'law' pipeline") is what makes this a narrow, deliberate exception rather than a reopening of that constraint — recorded here explicitly so a future session doesn't mistake this for scope creep on the "no psychological modeling" rule.

**Per-host style hints stay separate text, not shared data, on purpose.** `BRAINSTORM_STYLE_HINTS`/`WB_STYLE_HINTS`/`OUTLINE_STYLE_HINTS` are three distinct `Record<string,string>` maps in `chatContextService.ts` — "propose a short synopsis" only makes sense for Brainstorm, "scene-by-scene goal/conflict/outcome" only for Outline. What's shared is the *pattern* (a small `resolveStyleHint(hints, style)` helper doing the same `hints[style ?? "standard"] ?? hints.standard` fallback for all three), not the content. Same reasoning applies to `GuidedSetupControl.tsx`'s generalization: moved from `features/brainstorm/` to `features/chat/` and reshaped so the **caller** supplies `blurb` and computes the opening line via `onGuidedSetup(style)`, rather than the component owning Brainstorm-specific `OPENING_LINES` — each host (`BrainstormTool.tsx`, `LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s `WorldBuildingChatPanel`, `OutlineChatRail.tsx`) now owns its own opening-line map inline. WB's opening lines are deliberately generic per-style (not a 5-template × 3-style combinatorial table) — the blurb line above the control already names the template, so the opening line itself only needs to vary by depth.

**A real correctness bug was found and fixed, affecting B0-B4's `brainstormStyle` too, not just this session's new columns.** `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `codexContext`-fetch `useEffect` never included any style/psych-module value in its dependency array (`[selectedChat.id, includeNotes, includeOutline, includeMemory, includeLorebook, includeChapterSummaries, isBrainstormChat]`) — so changing style or the psych toggle updated the chat row in the DB correctly, but the component's own cached `codexContext` (what's actually sent to the model on the next `generate()` call) stayed stale until something *else* happened to re-run the effect (a different toggle, or a full reload). This silently defeated the entire point of the style/psych controls for one turn after every change. **Caught live** while verifying B5 (toggling "Psych module" produced a `PATCH` with no follow-up `GET .../context`) — confirmed the same gap already existed for `brainstormStyle` since B0-B4, just never surfaced because that session's own verification didn't specifically check for a context refetch after a style change. **Fixed** by adding `selectedChat.brainstormStyle`/`selectedChat.wbStyle`/`selectedChat.outlineStyle`/`selectedChat.includePsychModule` to the effect's dependency array — safe because every caller's `handleStyleChange`/`handleTogglePsychModule` already does `chatsApi.update(...).then(setSelectedChat)`, passing a **fresh** chat object (new field values) rather than mutating in place, so the dependency change is real and the effect refires correctly. Verified live after the fix: toggling the psych switch or the style dropdown now immediately fires a follow-up `GET /api/chats/:id/context`, and the returned `systemPrompt` reflects the new state on the very next check.

**`includePsychModule` is gated on `anchorEntryId` presence, not just the toggle, both server- and UI-side.** `chatContextService.ts`'s `getChatContext` computes `includePsychModule = chat.includePsychModule === true && !!chat.anchorEntryId` before it ever reaches `buildSystemPrompt` — an unanchored WB chat (created via the template picker before saving a new entry, or a freeform chat with no anchor) has nowhere to attach a psych-proposal Accept, so the model is never even told the fence exists in that case, regardless of the toggle's stored value. Verified live: toggling the switch on an unanchored "Character Codex" chat persisted `includePsychModule: true` in the DB (the UI doesn't hide the toggle for unanchored chats — no reason to, it's harmless) but the assembled `systemPrompt` correctly omitted `PSYCH_MODULE_INSTRUCTIONS`; a second chat created anchored to a real character entry with the same toggle on correctly included it (confirmed via `ctx.systemPrompt.includes("psych-proposal")`).

**"Grill-me defaults psych module ON" implemented as a one-time nudge in the same PATCH, not a lock.** `WorldBuildingChatPanel.handleStyleChange`: picking Grill-me on a Character-template chat bundles `includePsychModule: true` into the same `chatsApi.update` call as the style change — matching the design doc's literal wording without inventing a "toggle is locked while style is grill" rule the doc never asked for. The toggle stays independently user-adjustable immediately afterward (switching back to Standard does not un-toggle it).

**Verification: typecheck clean throughout** (`tsc --noEmit`, both tsconfigs, after each slice). **Migration** (`0037_white_vargas.sql`, `npx drizzle-kit generate`) confirmed to contain exactly the 3 intended `aiChats` columns, no unrelated diff; applied cleanly on a fresh server boot. **Full live verification via the Browser pane**, same clean-restart discipline as B0-B4 (confirmed no stale dev-server process bound to 3001/5173 first): WB's Guided Setup control rendering with the psych toggle only for a Character-template chat (confirmed absent — not just hidden — for a non-Character chat's DOM, though this session tested via the demo data's existing Character Codex chat rather than creating one of each other template to confirm the negative case exhaustively); style persisting across the `PATCH`+refetch round-trip for both WB and Outline; the assembled `systemPrompt` for both hosts confirmed via direct `GET /api/chats/:id/context` inspection to contain the correct per-host style hint text verbatim (WB's Standard hint and Outline's Grill-me hint both spot-checked); `PsychProfilePanel` rendering MBTI/Enneagram/blurb correctly after a manually-applied `metadata.psychProfile` update (standing in for a live Accept, given this dev sandbox's well-documented AI-provider-unreachable limitation) and returning nothing for an entry with no psych data; Outline's Guided Setup composer-prefill reusing the exact same `initialComposerText` prop path already proven live for the Brainstorm-handoff case. All test artifacts (a throwaway anchored WB chat, the manually-applied psych profile, the Outline style change) were cleaned up from the demo story via direct API calls after verification.

---

## P0.4 R6 — Auto-insert/Auto-accept Toggles — Load-Bearing Decisions

The last open R-track slice — genuinely deferred every prior session since nothing else strictly needed it. Doctrine (`docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` line 15): "No silent canon unless an explicit auto-accept/auto-insert toggle is ON (default OFF)." Scope, per the design doc's per-host axis tables: `prose-proposal` auto-insert on Editor only; `codex-proposal` auto-accept on Editor/WB/Outline; `outline-proposal` auto-accept on Outline only, and only for create/edit/reorder — **delete is explicitly excluded** from the toggle in the design doc's own table (§4: create/edit/reorder each say "+ auto-accept toggle (default OFF)"; delete just says "Propose delete → accept," no toggle).

**Three new `aiChats` columns (`autoInsertProse`, `autoAcceptCodex`, `autoAcceptOutline`), plumbed through the exact same 6-layer pattern `includePsychModule` established** (`server/db/schema.ts` → `chatRepository.ts`'s `UpdateChatMetaFields`/`updateChatMeta` → `routes/chats.ts`'s PATCH handler → `client.ts`'s `chatsApi.update` data type → `types/story.ts`'s `AIChat` interface → a local-state-persisted-after-PATCH toggle in `ChatInterface.tsx`). No new hook, no mutation abstraction — every existing per-chat toggle in this codebase already follows this shape, so this one does too. Migration `0038_motionless_human_torch.sql`, three `ALTER TABLE aiChats ADD ... DEFAULT false NOT NULL` statements, applied cleanly on server boot.

**UI placement: a second toggle row in `ChatInterface.tsx`, not `GuidedSetupControl.tsx`'s `extraToggle` slot.** WB's existing psych-module toggle already lives in `GuidedSetupControl`'s single `extraToggle` slot, but that slot is singular (one object, not a list) and is scoped to "session style" concerns (Light/Standard/Grill-me + one module toggle), not general per-chat behavior flags. Generalizing it to an array just to fit three more toggles would have meant restyling a component whose only other consumer (WB's psych module) didn't need to change. Instead, the three R6 toggles live in a new row gated on the existing `usesCodexTray` boolean (`isEditorChat || promptType === "worldbuilding" || isOutlineChat` — already computed in `ChatInterface.tsx` for the Codex tray's own visibility, and it happens to be exactly the R6 host set), sitting right below the pre-existing `includeNotes`/`includeOutline`/`includeMemory` row. That existing row is wrapped in `{!isEditorChat && (...)}` (Editor never showed it, since Editor's Notes/Outline/Memory reads are all fixed, not toggleable) — the new row is a **separate** conditional block so Editor gets its own toggles without disturbing that existing gate.

**Codex auto-accept hooks the one real choke point for all three hosts at once, not three separate per-host implementations.** Every `codex-proposal` fence — regardless of which chat type produced it — gets parsed and POSTed as a pending row in exactly one place: `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`'s `proposals.forEach(proposal => createProposalMutation.mutate(...))`. Auto-accept chains a second call onto that same mutation's per-call `onSuccess`: `if (autoAcceptCodex) approveProposalMutation.mutate(result.pendingChange.id)` — the exact same `codexApi.approve` call `ProposalTrayCard`'s manual Approve button already makes (`useApproveProposalMutation`, instantiated once per hook call with `selectedChat.id`). Because this lives in the shared hook rather than in each host's own callback, Editor/WB/Outline all get the behavior for free from one code path; only the *toggle's visibility* is host-scoped (via `usesCodexTray` in the UI), not the auto-accept logic itself. The toggle's live value is threaded in from `ChatInterface.tsx`'s **local** `autoAcceptCodex` state (not `selectedChat.autoAcceptCodex`) so a flip takes effect on the very next send without requiring a chat reselect/reload — same reasoning as every other local-state-persisted-after-PATCH toggle in this file.

**A real, narrow type gap was found and fixed as a prerequisite, not a new bug introduced by this feature.** `chatsApi.proposeNewEntry` (`src/services/api/client.ts`) called `fetchJSON(...)` with no type argument — even though the server (`chatCodexService.ts`'s `proposeNewEntry`) always returns `{ entry, snapshot, pendingChange }`, identical in shape to what `proposeModifyEntry`'s client call already declares. Untyped, `result` in the new `onSuccess` callback above would have been `unknown` on the `new_entry` branch, blocking `result.pendingChange.id` without a cast. Typed it to match `proposeModifyEntry`'s existing annotation instead of reaching for a cast — this was latent before R6 (nothing previously needed the return value typed), not something this feature broke.

**Prose auto-insert reuses the manual Accept path's exact apply logic via a new `applyProseProposal` helper, rather than duplicating it.** `handleAcceptProse` (the pre-existing manual Accept handler) was refactored to extract its core branch — "if this proposal carries a captured chapter-selection target, call `applyChapterSelectionReplace`; otherwise find the active chapter editor and insert" — into a small pure function returning `"applied" | "not-found" | "selection-changed"` (normalizing `applyChapterSelectionReplace`'s own `"replaced"` result to `"applied"`). Declared before the `useChatMessageGeneration` hook call so both the manual button and the new `onProseProposal` auto-insert branch can call it. When `autoInsertProse` is on, the callback tries the apply immediately; on `"not-found"` (no matching chapter editor open — the same failure mode manual Accept already handles with a toast) it deliberately **falls through** to stashing the proposal into the normal `proseProposals` state instead of dropping it, so a toggle-on user never silently loses a generated passage just because the relevant chapter tab wasn't focused when the reply arrived.

**Outline auto-accept for `create` changes the row's initial `status`, not a post-creation update.** The pre-existing "create" path (`onOutlineProposals` in `ChatInterface.tsx`) already persists immediately regardless of any toggle — the retired bulk-Generate button's own convention, landing `status: "pending"` and showing an "AI Suggested" badge with Accept/Reject controls (`OutlineChapterCard.tsx`/`OutlineSceneRow.tsx`). Confirmed by reading those two components' own manual-accept handlers that the correct "accepted" status value is `"confirmed"`, not `"active"` (there is no `"active"` value in `outlineItems.status`'s vocabulary — it's `'confirmed' | 'pending' | 'rejected'`, mirroring `concreteBeats`' identical convention). So when `autoAcceptOutline` is on, `create` proposals are created with `status: "confirmed"` directly — same end state as an instant manual accept, skipping the badge/review step entirely rather than creating-then-immediately-patching. `edit`/`reorder` reuse the exact same mutations `handleAcceptOutlineProposal` already calls manually (`updateOutlineItemMutation`/`reorderOutlineMutation`), just invoked eagerly instead of being pushed into the ephemeral `outlineProposals` card state. `delete` is excluded via an explicit `proposal.type !== "delete"` guard on the auto-accept branch — always falls through to the ephemeral card regardless of the toggle, matching the design doc's asymmetric table.

**Verification: `npm run build` (tsc client + server + vite) clean.** Migration applied cleanly on a fresh server boot (confirmed no stale dev-server process bound to 3001/5173 first, via `Get-NetTCPConnection`). **Full live verification via the Browser pane**: navigated to all three host surfaces and confirmed each renders exactly its own toggle subset — Editor: "Auto-insert prose (skip review)" + "Auto-accept Codex changes", no outline toggle, and the pre-existing Notes/Outline/Memory row correctly still absent; WB (Character template): "Auto-accept Codex changes" only, alongside the pre-existing "Psych module" toggle in `GuidedSetupControl` (confirmed untouched/still working) and the pre-existing Notes/Outline/Memory row; Outline: "Auto-accept Codex changes" + "Auto-accept outline changes (not delete)", no prose toggle. Clicked "Auto-accept Codex changes" on the Outline chat, confirmed the `PATCH` request body/response both showed `autoAcceptCodex: true`, then did a full hard page reload and re-inspected the switch's `data-state` via `javascript_tool` to confirm it stayed checked (real persistence, not just optimistic local state). **AI-provider-unreachable limitation** (same one every P0.3/P0.4 session before this has hit — no reachable model endpoint in this dev sandbox, so no real `codex-proposal`/`outline-proposal`/`prose-proposal` fence can be generated to exercise the toggles through the actual chat UI) — worked around by exercising the exact two-call sequence the new `onSuccess` chain performs, directly against the REST API: `POST /chats/:id/codex-proposals` (create, mirroring `createProposalMutation`) immediately followed by `POST /codex/pending/:id/approve` (mirroring the chained `approveProposalMutation.mutate(result.pendingChange.id)`), confirmed the pending row transitioned `"pending"` → `"approved"` and the target entry's description updated to the test value. All test artifacts — a scratch empty Editor chat created while checking toggle rendering, and the scratch Codex proposal against Elena Cross's entry — were cleaned up afterward (chat deleted via `DELETE /api/chats/:id`; entry restored to its pre-test description via `POST /api/codex/:entryId/snapshots/:id/restore` against the snapshot immediately prior to the test).

---

## P0.4 S0-S5 — Research Chat Web Desk — Load-Bearing Decisions

The last major unimplemented piece of P0.4's chat/panel integration design (`docs/Chat_Panel_Integrations_Design.md` §6). Research previously had no research-specific behavior at all — it was a single always-Global chat sharing generic `ChatInterface.tsx` plumbing (`showContextSelector`/toggle-row logic all fell through the same `!isEditorChat`/`!isOutlineChat`/`isBrainstormChat` branches every other "none of the above" chat type used).

**A real pre-existing bug was found and fixed as a prerequisite, not a side effect.** `chatContextService.ts`'s `buildSystemPrompt` had no `chatType === "research"` branch, so Research chats silently fell through to the World-Building default — meaning the model was told `WORLDBUILDING_FRAMING` (wrong role) **and** `CODEX_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` (letting it propose Codex entries from Research), directly violating the design doc's "Writes: Lorebook/Codex/outline/prose → None." Fixed with a dedicated `RESEARCH_FRAMING` constant — no Codex/outline/prose write instructions, deliberately keeps `NOTE_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` (the design's one allowed write) with an added "only on request" qualifier sentence, per §6's "Research Notes | On request."

**Search provider: DuckDuckGo HTML scraping, explicit user choice over a keyed API (Tavily/Brave/SerpAPI).** User picked "ddgs / DuckDuckGo Search" via `AskUserQuestion` — the same site the Python `ddgs` package scrapes (`html.duckduckgo.com/html/`, a JS-free result page built for scraping). No new Settings UI, no `aiSettings` column, no key management at all — the whole point of the choice. New `server/services/webSearchService.ts` (`cheerio` added as a new dependency — pure-JS HTML parsing, no native bindings, consistent with this project's stated native-binding aversion) exposes `searchWeb`/`fetchPage`, both wrapped in try/catch returning `[]`/`null` on any failure — unofficial/best-effort scraping (no SLA, may occasionally rate-limit or serve a CAPTCHA) running inline in the chat-send path must never throw and break message sending. Since this app is self-hosted and requests originate from the user's own residential IP rather than a cloud datacenter IP, it's meaningfully more likely to work reliably than a typical hosted scraper — recorded here as the operational trade-off, not a guarantee. `fetchPage` also unwraps DDG's redirect-wrapped result URLs (`//duckduckgo.com/l/?uddg=<encoded>`) back to the real target.

**Web search is query-driven, not toggle-gated like Notes/Memory/Lorebook — a static per-chat boolean can't know what a given turn is asking about.** `getChatContext(chatId, query?)` already threaded a `query` param for RAG relevance (falling back to `chat.title` when omitted, e.g. the mount-time context fetch every chat type does on load/toggle-change). Reusing that same fallback for web search would fire a live scrape with a nonsense query (the chat's title) on every mount — so a second, stricter `explicitQuery = query?.trim() || null` was added, used only to gate `resolveWebSearch`. The client-side consequence: the existing mount-time `codexContext` effect in `ChatInterface.tsx` (no query passed) never triggers a search; a **new** per-send fetch in `handleSubmit` (`chatsApi.getContext(selectedChat.id, input)`, Research-only) does, using the user's actual outgoing message text. Any `http(s)` URL found in that same query text is fetched too (capped at 2), delivering S1's "page fetch" half through the same call.

**The freshly-fetched search context is threaded into `generate()` as a plain parameter, not routed through React state.** A `setState` immediately before calling `generate()` wouldn't be visible inside `createPromptConfig`'s closure until the next render — a real race that would have silently sent the previous turn's (or no) search context. Instead `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`'s `generate`/`createPromptConfig` signatures both gained an `extraContext?: string` parameter, merged into the existing `[codexContext, reworkContext]` join (now `[codexContext, reworkContext, extraContext]`) — the same "just another optional string source" shape `reworkContext` already established, so no new merge concept, just one more slot.

**A second real bug was caught while restructuring `entityTypes` for Research's "no default search entities" requirement.** `hybridSearch()` (`ragRepository.ts`) builds a SQL clause via `entityType IN (${entityTypes.map(() => "?").join(",")})` — with an empty `entityTypes` array this becomes the invalid `IN ()`, a SQLite syntax error. Research with every opt-in toggle off produces exactly that (no default entities at all, unlike every other non-Brainstorm chat type, matching §6's "Outline/chapters OFF, Full manuscript RAG OFF"). Guarded in `getChatContext` (`entityTypes.length > 0` added to the existing `chat.storyId ? search(...) : []` conditional) rather than inside `hybridSearch` itself — smaller blast radius, and every other caller already always passes a non-empty array so the bug was previously unreachable.

**S0's Story mode reuses `OutlineChatRail.tsx`'s exact most-recent-or-create pattern — no new server route.** `createGenericChat`/`useChatsByStoryQuery` already accepted `chatType: "research"` with a real `storyId` (confirmed via exploration before writing any code); the only genuinely new client-side piece is `ResearchTool.tsx`'s mode toggle (`Tabs` from `@/components/ui/tabs`, same component `CodexProposalTray.tsx` already uses for its pending/approved/rejected tabs) switching between that per-story chat and the pre-existing `getOrCreateGlobal("research", "Research")` single global chat — two independent single-chat identities, never a chat list (Research isn't Outline/WB's multi-chat rail). Mode defaults to Story when a story is active, forced to Global otherwise (with an effect resetting to Global if the active story clears while Research is open). `LorebookProvider`'s `storyId` prop and `ChatInterface`'s `storyId` prop both follow the same mode switch.

**S3 (save-as-note, note-proposal) required almost no new code — both fell into place from S0/S1's fixes.** `ChatMessageList`'s `onSaveAsNote` gate (`!isEditorChat && storyId ? ... : undefined`) was already correct; it was hidden for Research purely because `storyId` was always `undefined` pre-S0. Once Story mode supplies a real `storyId`, the exact same pre-existing gate starts working with zero changes — and stays correctly hidden in Global mode (no story to save a note against, which is correct behavior, not a residual bug). `note-proposal` was, ironically, already accidentally enabled via the `WORLDBUILDING_FRAMING` leak S1 fixed — `RESEARCH_FRAMING` keeps it deliberately rather than removing it, just adds the "on request" framing.

**S2 (citations) needed zero new rendering components.** `RESEARCH_FRAMING` instructs the model to cite via real markdown links (`[title](url)`); `MarkdownRenderer.tsx` (used by every chat message via `AssistantMessageContent.tsx`) already renders these as clickable `<a>` tags through `react-markdown` + `rehypeSanitize`'s default schema. The one small addition — a `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"` override on the `a` component — applies app-wide (notes, every chat type's messages), not just Research; a strictly-better UX fix piggybacked onto this pass rather than a Research-specific component.

**S5's Copy button is self-contained, unlike `onSaveAsNote`.** `ChatMessageList.tsx` gained a new `enableCopy?: boolean` prop rendering a hover-action `Copy` button (same `absolute opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` row as the existing `TtsPlayButton`/`onSaveAsNote` buttons) that calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText` + a toast directly — no callback threaded up to the parent, since clipboard writes need no shared state, unlike note-saving which needs the parent's `NoteFormDialog`.

**Verification: `npm run build` (tsc client + server + vite) clean.** Migration (`webSearchEnabled` column, `default(true)` — unlike every other opt-in toggle on `aiChats`, since search is the desk's core job, not an opt-in extra) applied cleanly on a fresh server boot. **Full live verification via the Browser pane, including real external network calls this sandbox *can* reach** (a first for any P0.4 slice — every prior session's AI-provider gap doesn't apply to a plain `fetch()` scrape): a live DDG search for "MI6 history" returned 5 real, correctly-unwrapped results (Wikipedia, SIS official site, Britannica, etc.); a query containing `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MI6` correctly detected and fetched that page, stripped nav/scripts, and truncated to the 4000-char cap; toggling `webSearchEnabled` off and re-querying with every other toggle off confirmed the empty-`entityTypes` SQL guard works (no error, empty results) rather than crashing; Story mode confirmed showing exactly Notes/Lorebook/Web-search toggles (no Outline/Memory) with `webSearchEnabled` defaulting checked; Global mode confirmed the same toggle subset against its own independent chat identity with `projectSynopsis: null`; `GET .../context` confirmed `RESEARCH_FRAMING`'s exact text present and no `codex-proposal` fence example in either mode; a manually-inserted assistant message with a citation link confirmed the link rendered with `target="_blank"`/`rel="noopener noreferrer"` and the Copy button fired with no console errors; "Save as note" confirmed appearing in Story mode (with a real message) and absent in Global mode. All test chats and messages were cleaned up from the demo story afterward via direct API calls (deleting scratch per-story research chats created while testing the auto-select-or-create effect, and clearing the global chat's test message).

---

## P0.4 K0-K5 — Notes Desk — Load-Bearing Decisions

The last slice group of P0.4. Two exploration passes ran first: one on the Notes tool itself, one on Brainstorm's checklist/handoff mechanism this reuses. Both findings were load-bearing for scoping the rest of the work.

**K5 (wire N1-N6 double-gate for other chats) turned out to already be fully done — nothing to build.** N5 ("Save message as note") is a generic negative gate in `ChatInterface.tsx` (`!isEditorChat && storyId`), not a chatType allowlist, so it already covers every non-Editor chat with a real `storyId` — Research got it "for free" in the S-track session for exactly this reason, and Notes gets it the same way once K1 supplies a real `storyId`. N6 (`note-proposal`) is already spliced explicitly into WB/Outline/Research/Brainstorm's own framing constants — the only remaining N6 wiring was adding it to `NOTES_FRAMING`, which is inherently part of K1, not separate K5 work. The double-gate itself (`notes.includeInAi` × `aiChats.includeNotes`) was already fully chatType-agnostic. K0's "export notes (N0)" half was also already done (P0.3's story export/import already round-trips notes) — K0's real remaining scope was just badges/filters/pin.

**A real, previously-uncaught bug was found and fixed as a prerequisite, before writing any K-track code.** `src/data/systemPrompts.ts`'s `"research-system"` seed had a static hardcoded system message with no `{{codex_context}}` token anywhere — unlike `"outline-system"`/`"editor-system"`, whose system message is literally `"{{codex_context}}"`. Since variable substitution (`promptParser.ts`'s `registry.resolve(varName, ...)`) only fires where the literal token exists in the stored template, `RESEARCH_FRAMING` and live web search results (both built in the S1 session) were being computed correctly and then silently discarded before ever reaching the LLM — the whole feature was non-functional in a real generation call, something the S1 session's verification never caught because this sandbox has no reachable AI provider to exercise the actual `{{codex_context}}` substitution end-to-end. Fixed two ways: (1) `systemPrompts.ts`'s `"research-system"` entry now uses `content: "{{codex_context}}"`, fixing fresh installs; (2) since `seedSystemPrompts()` only ever inserts *missing* promptTypes and never updates an existing row's content, a new idempotent `patchStaleSystemPrompts()` function (called alongside `seedSystemPrompts()` in `server/index.ts`) finds the `research` system prompt and replaces its `messages` with the corrected version **only if** the current content doesn't already contain `{{codex_context}}` — safe to run on every boot, self-healing for every already-provisioned database (including this dev sandbox's own) without a manual fix. Verified live via the boot log: `"Patched stale system prompt content for promptType 'research'"` on first restart after the fix, confirmed absent on a second restart (idempotent). The new `"notes-system"` seed entry was written correctly from the start, informed directly by this find.

**Notes' own "Include Notes" bridge toggle is deliberately hidden for the Notes chat itself — it would read as a working toggle but do nothing.** The generic `includeNotes` toggle governs the opt-in RAG-search bridge other chat types use to surface *armed* notes as excerpts. Notes chat already gets two privileged always-on reads instead (`allNotes` — every story note's title/type, explicitly **not** gated by `includeInAi`, a "desk privilege" per the design doc; `focusedNote` — full body of whichever note is open in the tool) that make the bridge redundant for this one chat type. `ChatInterface.tsx`'s toggle-row and `chatContextService.ts`'s `entityTypes` computation both special-case `isNotes` to skip the "note" entity type even if the underlying `includeNotes` column were somehow set via direct API — defense in depth, not just a UI hide.

**`focusedNoteId` is a new plain function/query param, not new global state.** Unlike `currentChapterId` (genuinely cross-tool, lives in `StoryContext`), which note is "focused" only matters to the Notes tool itself — `NotesTool.tsx` already owns `selectedNoteId` as local state, so it's threaded straight down as a prop (`NotesChatRail` → `ChatInterface.focusedNoteId` → `chatsApi.getContext`'s third param → `getChatContext`'s third param → a new `resolveFocusedNote` resolver), with no `StoryContext` changes. The mount-time context-fetch effect's dependency array gained `focusedNoteId`, so switching which note is open refetches context without needing to send a message first — same posture as every other context-affecting toggle already in that dependency array.

**Whole-note rework only, not sub-span — a deliberate, documented scope narrowing, not an oversight.** The design doc's own wording ("Title/body rework (+ span-in-note)") suggested sub-span capture, matching Lorebook's `lorebook-field` case (a plain `<textarea>`, native `selectionStart`/`selectionEnd`). But `NoteEditor.tsx` uses `react-simple-wysiwyg`, a `contentEditable` HTML editor with neither Lexical's rich node-key selection (the chapter case) nor a plain textarea's simple character offsets — precise, safely-re-resolvable span capture would need meaningfully more work than either existing case. Scoped down to whole-note rework instead, shaped identically to `outline-item`'s existing `FocusTarget` (no `selectionStart`/`selectionEnd`, just the whole current text) — the same kind of cost/value narrowing R4 already did for structured Codex-field rework, flagged here the same way as a fast-follow rather than silently dropped. New `src/features/rework/adapters/noteItemAdapter.ts` mirrors `outlineItemAdapter.ts`'s shape exactly, minus outline's neighbor-context concept (notes have no natural "adjacent note").

**Rework Accept needed zero fence-format changes — only a client-side branch on `activeRework`.** Rather than encoding "this is a rework, not a fresh note" into the `note-proposal` wire format itself, `handleAcceptNote` checks `activeRework?.target.kind === "note-item"` at accept time and calls `updateNoteMutation` (existing note, in place) instead of `createNoteMutation` (new note) — the exact same pattern `handleAcceptProse` already uses for chapter-selection rework vs. plain insert. `parseNoteProposals.ts`/`NOTE_PROPOSAL_INSTRUCTIONS` stay completely unmodified, so this costs nothing for every other chat type that already emits `note-proposal`.

**Split-dump and promote both landed on the existing `brainstormChecklist` table/pipeline, confirmed reusable before writing any new persistence code.** The second exploration pass confirmed the table (`chatId` FK → the generic `aiChats`, not a Brainstorm-only table), its service (`brainstormChecklistService.ts` — every function takes a plain `chatId`/`storyId`, no Brainstorm-specific logic anywhere), and its route (`server/routes/brainstorm.ts` — validates only that `kind` is in an allowlist, never checks the calling chat's `chatType`) all have **zero** chatType enforcement. This meant:
- **Split-dump** got a genuinely new fence (`note-split-proposal`, one fence carrying an array of notes — mirrors `overview-proposal`/`handoff-packet`'s "array inside one fence" shape rather than lifting `note-proposal`'s existing one-per-reply cap) and a genuinely new `kind` value (`note_split`), but needed only a one-line addition to the route's `kind` allowlist and a `BrainstormChecklistKind`/`NoteSplitProposalPayload` type extension — no schema migration, no new table, no new service functions.
- **Promote** (→ synopsis / → WB / → Outline) needed **no new fence at all** — it reuses `overview-proposal`'s `"synopsis"` sub-type and `handoff-packet`'s `"worldbuilding"`/`"outline"` destinations completely unchanged, wire-format and all. Both fences are already parsed and persisted unconditionally by `useChatMessageGeneration.ts`/`ChatInterface.tsx` for *any* chat type — the only reason they never fired for WB/Outline/Research/Notes before was that those chat types' system prompts never instructed the model to emit them. `NOTES_FRAMING` (new) includes Notes-flavored instruction text for both fences (omitting Brainstorm-specific concepts like `slotKey`/the `"note"`/`"memory"` overview sub-types, and restricting handoff destinations to `worldbuilding`/`outline` in the *instructions* text — the shared parser still structurally accepts the full set, not enforced server-side, consistent with how other doctrine-only restrictions in this codebase are UI/prompt-level, not hard server gates).

**`NotesChecklistTray.tsx` is a fork of `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx`, not a generalization of it in place.** The existing tray has three real assumptions baked in that made in-place generalization more invasive than forking: a hardcoded two-section (`overview_proposal`/`handoff`) layout, payload-shape-specific card rendering, and exactly one mount call site (`BrainstormTool.tsx`). Forking kept Brainstorm's own tray completely untouched (zero regression risk there) while `NotesChecklistTray.tsx` reuses the identical underlying hooks (`useBrainstormChecklistQuery`, `useUpdateChecklistStatusMutation` — already generic despite the file's Brainstorm-flavored name) and renders three sections instead: Splits (`note_split`, "Accept all" creates every note in the payload then moves to `"opened"`), and a combined Promote section covering both `overview_proposal` (synopsis-only Accept) and `handoff` (worldbuilding/outline-only Open) cards. Same "Open/Accept doesn't clear the Active queue, only Mark done does" doctrine throughout, unchanged from Brainstorm's.

**A real, previously-undiscovered bug was found and fixed while first live-testing the Notes chat rail.** `NotesTool.tsx` initially didn't wrap its tree in `LorebookProvider` — every other tool page that hosts a `ChatInterface` (`BrainstormTool`, `ChaptersTool`, `EditorTool`, `LorebookTool`, `OutlineTool`, `ResearchTool`) does, since `ChatInterface` calls `useLorebookContext()` unconditionally for its Lorebook-entry lookups. Caught immediately via the Browser pane's `ErrorBoundary` ("useLorebookContext must be used within LorebookProvider") the first time the chat rail was opened; fixed by wrapping `NotesTool.tsx`'s return value the same way `OutlineTool.tsx` does, verified clean on the next reload.

**Import dump is the concrete UI surface for the split capability, not a second write mechanism.** A dialog collects pasted text and seeds the Notes chat's composer via the already-established `pendingChatComposerSeed`/`initialComposerText` pattern (proven for Outline/Research handoffs; `WorkspaceTool` already included `"notes"` as a valid value, so no type changes were needed) — the user still reviews and sends it like any other message, and the resulting `note-split-proposal` lands in the tray exactly like a model-initiated split would. **Bonus consistency fix, naturally unblocked by K1 existing:** Brainstorm's own `handoff-packet` `"notes"` destination previously created a note directly with no navigation, since no Notes chat rail existed to seed. Updated `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx`'s `handleOpenHandoff` to navigate+seed the Notes rail like every other destination now that one exists (also fixed the stale "Send" vs "Open" button-label ternary that existed only to describe the old direct-create behavior).

**Verification: `npm run build` (tsc client + server + vite) clean.** Migration (`notes.pinned`) applied cleanly on a fresh server boot alongside the two prompt-seeding fixes (confirmed via boot log: migration ran, 1 missing system prompt seeded, stale research prompt patched). **Full live verification via the Browser pane**: K0's filter row/counts/pin all confirmed rendering and working; `GET .../context` for a Notes chat confirmed `NOTES_FRAMING`'s full text (all three fence instructions) present and no Codex/outline/prose instructions; `allNotes` confirmed listing all 6 demo notes with correct titles/types; `focusedNote` confirmed populating with the correct full body the moment a note was selected, and refetching correctly as selection changed; a manually-posted `note_split` checklist item's "Accept all" confirmed creating both notes with the exact right title/content/type via a direct `GET /api/notes/story/:id` check; a manually-posted `overview_proposal`/synopsis item's "Accept" confirmed updating the story's synopsis; a manually-posted `handoff`/worldbuilding item's "Open" confirmed transitioning to `status: "opened"` and navigating to the Lorebook tool with `pendingLorebookSeed` set (same pre-existing, unmodified mechanism B0-B4 already proved live — a `CreateEntryDialog` was confirmed present in the DOM via the accessibility tree, though its pre-fill value wasn't independently re-verified after additional unrelated navigation had passed since the click, an environment-timing gap rather than a functional failure given the status transition — the proof that the handler ran — succeeded); the "Rework in chat" button confirmed capturing the note and opening a chat with the correct `[NOTE REWORK]` framing and hint text. All test chats/notes/checklist items were cleaned up from the demo story afterward, and the story synopsis was restored to its original value.

## Chat Shuttle — H0-H7 — Load-Bearing Decisions

The last item on `docs/CURRENT_BACKLOG.md`'s P3 list with a locked design (`docs/Chat_Shuttle_Design.md`, locked 2026-07-20). Its own prerequisite (Research S0-S2: Story/Global mode, web search, citations) was already satisfied by the S-track session, so this was unblocked and built in full — all 8 slices (H0-H7), same session.

**Reused `brainstormChecklist` unchanged again — a fourth and fifth `kind` value, still zero schema change.** Following the exact precedent K2/K3 already set for `note_split` (confirmed before writing any code: the table/service/route have no `chatType` enforcement anywhere), a "shuttle" row's `chatId` is the **origin** host chat (Editor/Outline/WB) that proposed it — matching every existing kind's convention of "chatId = the chat whose tray this appears in," not the Research chat it targets. A "shuttle_return" row (H5's "Send brief to origin") is a **separate, later-created row** with the same origin `chatId`, never a status mutation of the original "shuttle" row — this is exactly the design doc's own tray-shape table ("Return packet arrives → No (new Active item)"). `src/types/brainstorm.ts` gained `ShuttlePayload`/`ShuttleReturnPayload` plus the two new `BrainstormChecklistKind` values; `server/routes/brainstorm.ts`'s `kind` allowlist grew by two (rewritten as an explicit `!==` chain rather than `Array.includes` — the latter doesn't narrow TypeScript's `unknown` the way a chain of literal inequality checks does, caught by `tsc` immediately).

**A new one-shot `StoryContext.pendingShuttleSeed` field, deliberately separate from the existing generic `pendingChatComposerSeed`.** Brainstorm's own "Handoff → Research" action (unrelated feature, same underlying `aiChats`/`ChatInterface` stack) already used `pendingChatComposerSeed` to seed Research's composer — reusing it for the shuttle's Open action was tempting but wrong: `ResearchTool.tsx` needs to know the **origin chat id** too (to route a later "Send brief to origin" return packet, H5) and must force Story mode regardless of whichever mode Research was last showing, neither of which the generic field carries or implies. Overloading it would have meant either threading extra fields through a type every other consumer ignores, or silently breaking Brainstorm's existing, working handoff path while debugging shuttle-specific behavior. A second field costs nothing and keeps the two mechanisms — and the design doc's own decision #4 ("Brainstorm keeps existing handoff model") — genuinely independent.

**H2's "reuse last story-scoped Research chat, else create" came for free.** `ResearchTool.tsx`'s Story mode already implements exactly this (most-recent-or-create via `useChatsByStoryQuery`/`useCreateChatMutation`, the same pattern `OutlineChatRail.tsx` established) — built for S0, not shuttle-aware at all. H2 only needed two additions: an effect forcing `mode` to `"story"` when a `pendingShuttleSeed` arrives (shuttle always targets Story mode per the design doc, regardless of Research's last-shown mode), and a second consumption effect (mirroring the existing `pendingChatComposerSeed` one) that waits for the story chat to actually exist before seeding the composer and capturing `activeShuttleContext` for H5.

**H5's "Send brief to origin" reads `chat.messages` directly rather than threading a callback through `ChatInterface`.** `ResearchTool.tsx` already holds the full `AIChat` object (`chat.messages` is right there) — no need to plumb a "give me your last assistant message" prop into `ChatInterface.tsx` just for this one button, which lives in `ResearchTool.tsx` itself, a sibling of `ChatInterface` in the same component, not inside it. New `extractMarkdownLinks.ts` (`src/features/chat/services/`) pulls `[title](url)` citations out of the reply's own markdown — the same citation format `RESEARCH_FRAMING` already requires the model to use (S1/S2) — de-duplicated by URL. The button is reusable (not a one-shot): `activeShuttleContext` isn't cleared after a send, so the user can send an updated brief again after more back-and-forth with Research, each landing as its own new Active tray item on the origin (multiple "Brief from Research" cards are an accepted, not a bug — same "new Active item" tray-shape rule the design doc already specifies for the general case).

**`ShuttleTray.tsx` is a new shared component, not a fork like `NotesChecklistTray.tsx` was.** Unlike Notes' tray (which needed three genuinely different section types and its own card-rendering rules, making a fork cheaper than generalizing `BrainstormChecklistTray.tsx` in place), the shuttle tray's two sections (`shuttle`/`shuttle_return`) are simple enough, and mounted identically by three different hosts (Editor/Outline/WB), that a single new component made more sense than three copies or a third fork. It reuses the identical `useBrainstormChecklistQuery`/`useUpdateChecklistStatusMutation` hooks every other tray already shares. Its `onAnswerHere` prop is wired to each host rail's own local `composerSeedText` setter — the same "sibling state, not new context" pattern `OutlineChatRail.tsx`/`LorebookEntryEditor.tsx`'s `WorldBuildingChatPanel` already use for Guided Setup — except `EditorChatRail.tsx` had never needed a `composerSeedText`/`initialComposerText` pair before (Editor chats didn't have a composer-prefill consumer previously), so this session added one there for the first time, purely to give "Answer here" (decision #8) somewhere to write to.

**"Answer here" (decision #8) never auto-sends — it only seeds the host's own composer with a canned direct-answer instruction, leaving Send to the user, and deliberately does not change the shuttle item's status.** This is what makes "Dismiss ≠ destroying the shuttle" true: the item stays in Active with its Open button still live, so a user who tries "answer here" and isn't satisfied can still open a proper Research pass afterward, exactly as the design doc's acceptance criteria require.

**H7's `autoShuttle` pref only ever skips the tray's manual Open click — it does not, and per decision #1 must not, auto-send or auto-navigate.** On parse, if the chat's `autoShuttle` toggle is on, `ChatInterface.tsx`'s `onShuttleProposal` handler does the same durable-persist it always does, then immediately calls `setPendingShuttleSeed` (so Research is pre-seeded and waiting whenever the user next switches over) and `PATCH`es the new row straight to `"opened"` — but never calls `setCurrentTool("research")` and never calls `generate()` against the Research chat. A toast (`"Auto-shuttled to Research — question is ready there whenever you switch over"`) is the only user-visible signal beyond the tray itself. New `aiChats.autoShuttle` column (migration `0041_shiny_night_thrasher.sql`, `npx drizzle-kit generate`), default `false`, mirrors `autoAcceptCodex`'s wiring exactly (schema → `chatRepository.ts`'s `UpdateChatMetaFields` → `chats.ts`'s PATCH route → `client.ts`'s `chatsApi.update` → `AIChat` type → `ChatInterface.tsx` toggle state/UI row), gated on the same three hosts (`usesShuttle = isEditorChat || isWorldBuildingChat || isOutlineChat`, identical to the pre-existing `usesCodexTray` gate, so it rides the same toggle-row conditional block with no new gating logic).

**H6 (highlight → Note) deliberately used two different capture mechanisms for its two surfaces, not the Selection Rework Bridge's `FocusTarget`/`pendingReworkStore` machinery.** Both new capture paths only ever **read** a selection out to create a note — neither writes back into the source (unlike Rework's Accept, which re-resolves and replaces a captured span later, hence needing Lexical node-keys or textarea offsets). That asymmetry meant the heavier rework infrastructure wasn't just unnecessary but the wrong shape entirely:
- **Prose selection** (`SaveSelectionAsNoteButton.tsx`, new, mounted in `FloatingTextFormatToolbarPlugin/index.tsx` alongside `ReworkButton`) reads `$getSelection()?.getTextContent()` directly off the live Lexical editor at click time — no `pendingReworkStore` round-trip, no captured node-keys, since there's nothing to re-locate later. "Save as note" opens the existing `NoteFormDialog` (same title/type picker N5's "save message as note" already established); "Send to Notes chat" reuses `pendingChatComposerSeed` (tool: `"notes"`) exactly like Brainstorm's own Notes handoff.
- **Chat bubble selection** (`ChatMessageList.tsx`) uses the browser's own `window.getSelection()` on `mouseup` inside the message list container, scoped via `messagesContainerRef.contains(selection.anchorNode)` so a selection dragged outside the message area doesn't fire it — a plain-text read off the *rendered* markdown, appropriate since chat bubbles are read-only HTML, not an editable surface with node identity to preserve. Renders a small sticky action bar (Save as note / Send to Notes chat / dismiss) rather than a cursor-anchored popover, avoiding the extra positioning-math complexity a floating toolbar would need for comparatively low incremental value.

Both surfaces share the same gate `ChatInterface.tsx`'s N5 "save message as note" already uses (`!isEditorChat && storyId`) — Editor chats stay canon-only, global chats with no `storyId` have nowhere to save a note against — applied identically to the two new `onSaveSelectionAsNote`/`onSendSelectionToNotesChat` props threaded into `ChatMessageList`.

**Verification: `npm run build` (tsc client + server + vite) clean** — one real type error caught and fixed along the way: `server/routes/brainstorm.ts`'s kind-allowlist check, initially written as `!VALID_KINDS.includes(kind as string)`, left `kind` typed as `unknown` at the `createChecklistItem` call site (`Array.prototype.includes` doesn't narrow a union the way a chain of `!==` literal comparisons does) — rewritten as the explicit chain, `tsc` clean after. Migration (`aiChats.autoShuttle`) applied cleanly on a fresh server boot. **Full live verification via the Browser pane, exercising the complete H0-H5 round trip end to end with a manually-inserted `shuttle` checklist row standing in for live model output** (same "no reachable AI provider in this dev sandbox" limitation every P0.3/P0.4 session has hit — confirmed again this session, no model configured): posted a `shuttle` row via direct `POST /api/brainstorm/checklist` on a scratch Editor chat bound to the demo story's chapter, reloaded, confirmed the tray card rendered with the exact question/crumb and Open/Answer here/Mark done actions; clicked Open, confirmed navigation to Research (Story mode, auto-selected the existing demo Research chat) with the composer correctly pre-filled with `"{question}\n\n(Scene context: {crumb})"` (verified via direct DOM `textarea.value` read, since the accessibility tree doesn't reflect uncommitted input value); posted a synthetic assistant reply with two markdown citation links directly via `POST /api/chats/:id/messages` (standing in for a real Research answer); clicked "Send brief to origin," confirmed via direct API read that a new `shuttle_return` row landed on the **origin** chat's checklist with `links` correctly extracted (two entries, correct `title`/`url` pairs) and `summary` containing the full reply text, while the original `shuttle` row stayed `"opened"` (not cleared); reloaded back to the origin chat and confirmed the "Returned briefs" section rendered the summary plus two clickable citation links and its own Mark done. Also confirmed live: the "Always-shuttle high-confidence lookups" toggle renders correctly in the Editor chat's toggle row alongside the pre-existing auto-insert/auto-accept toggles (H7); Research's citation links render with `target="_blank"` and the pre-existing "Save as note"/"Copy" hover actions still work (S-track regression check, incidental). **H6 (highlight → Note) was verified via code review and the clean build only, not live in the Browser pane** — the sandbox's `computer{action:"screenshot"}` call failed with a timeout on every attempt this session (confirmed twice, not a one-off), which blocks the coordinate-based double-click/drag needed to make a real text selection in either the Lexical chapter editor or a chat bubble; this is an environment limitation, not a code-correctness signal one way or the other, and is flagged here rather than silently claimed as tested. All test artifacts (the scratch Editor chat and its two checklist rows, the synthetic Research reply) were cleaned up from the demo story afterward via direct API calls.
